and
\psi
commute, and the composite of
\sigma
and
\tau
is the identity.

\textstyle{X^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{(\Pi_{A}B)^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\Pi_{A_{\epsilon}}B_{\epsilon}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\Pi_{A}B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{\Pi_{A^{\mathrm{I}}}B^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\Pi_{A_{\epsilon}}p_{*}p^{*}B_{\epsilon}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\textstyle{(\Pi_{A}B)^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\textstyle{\Pi_{A_{\epsilon}}B_{\epsilon}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Pi_{A}B}

(80)

To see that
\psi\circ\varphi=1
, an easy chase through the diagram (
80
) shows that

\epsilon\circ\psi\circ\varphi=p\circ\varphi=\epsilon\,.

Thus by applying
\mathrm{I}^{*}
and using (
78
) we have
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{I}^{*}}\circ\mathrm{I}^{*}(\psi\circ\varphi)=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{I}^{*}}
,
and so
\psi\circ\varphi=\widetilde{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{I}^{*}}}=1
.
∎

From Proposition
68
we then have:

Corollary 73

(Unbiased Frobenius)
.

The unbiased fibration weak factorization system on
\mathsf{cSet}
satisfies the Frobenius condition.

Corollary 74
.

Unbiased fibrations are closed under pushforward along unbiased fibrations. Thus given unbiased fibrations
X\twoheadrightarrow Z
and
Y\twoheadrightarrow Z
over any base
Z
, the relative exponential
Y^{X}=X_{*}X^{*}Y\rightarrow Z
, formed in the slice over
Z
, is again an unbiased fibration.

Remark 75
.

We note in passing that the proof just given for the
\delta
-biased case of Frobenius, Proposition
72
, made no use of the fact that
\delta:1\rightarrow\mathrm{I}
is generic, nor even that we were working in the slice category over
\mathrm{I}
. Indeed the same algebraic argument works for
p
-biased fibrations for any point
p:1\rightarrow\mathrm{I}
of any object
\mathrm{I}
, in any (quasi-)topos
\mathcal{E}
.

6.
A universal fibration

We shall construct a
universal small fibration

\dot{\mathcal{U}}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}
, which is a classifier for small fibrations. It will be shown in Section
8
that the base object
\mathcal{U}
is fibrant, using the fact to be proved in Section
7
that the map
\dot{\mathcal{U}}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}
itself is
univalent
, in a sense to be made precise.

Our construction of
\dot{\mathcal{U}}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}
makes use, first of all, of a new description of the well-known Hofmann-Streicher universe in a category
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}=[{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set}]
of presheaves on a small category
\mathbb{C}
, which was used in
[
HS97
]
to interpret dependent type theory. See
[
Awo22
]
for further details.

Classifying families

Definition 76

(
[
HS97
]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{C}
be a small category. A (type-theoretic)
universe

(U,{\mathsf{E}l})
consists of

U\in\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
and
\textstyle{\mathsf{E}l}\in\widehat{\int_{\mathbb{C}}U}
with:

\displaystyle U(c)\

\displaystyle=\ \mathsf{Cat}\big({\mathbb{C}/_{c}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set}\big)

(81)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{E}l}(c,A)\

\displaystyle=\ A(id_{c})

(82)

with the evident associated action on morphisms.

A few comments are required:

•

In contrast to
[
HS97
]
, in (
81
) we take the underlying set of objects of the functor category
\widehat{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}=[{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set}]
.

•

As in
[
HS97
]
, (
82
) adopts the “categories with families” point of view in describing an arrow
E\rightarrow U
in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
equivalently as a presheaf on the category of elements
\int_{\mathbb{C}}U
, using

\textstyle\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/_{U}\ \simeq\ \widehat{\int_{\mathbb{C}}U}\,

(83)

where

E(c)\ =\ {\textstyle\coprod_{A\in U(c)}{\mathsf{E}l}(c,A)}.

The argument
(c,A)\in\int_{\mathbb{C}}U
in (
82
) thus consists of an object
c\in\mathbb{C}
and an element
A\in U(c)
.

•

To account for size issues, the authors of
[
HS97
]
assume a Grothendieck universe
u
in
\mathsf{Set}
, the elements of which are called
small
. The category
\mathbb{C}
is assumed to be small, as are the values of the presheaves, unless otherwise stated.

The presheaf
U
, which is not small, is then regarded as the Grothendieck universe
u
“lifted” from
\mathsf{Set}
to
[{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set}]
. We first analyse this specification of
(U,{\mathsf{E}l})
from a different perspective, in order to establish its basic property as a classifier for small families in
\widehat{}\mathbb{C}
.

A realization-nerve adjunction.

For a presheaf
X
on
\mathbb{C}
, recall that the category of elements is the comma category,

\textstyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}X\ =\ \mathsf{y}_{\mathbb{C}}/_{X}\,,

where
\mathsf{y}_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathsf{Set}^{\mathbb{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}
is the Yoneda embedding, which we sometimes supress and write simply
\mathbb{C}/_{X}
for
\mathsf{y}_{\mathbb{C}}/_{X}
.

Proposition 77

(
[
Gro83
]
, §28)
.

The category of elements functor

\textstyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}:\widehat{}\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}

has a right adjoint,

\nu_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathsf{Cat}\longrightarrow\widehat{}\mathbb{C}\,.

For a small category
\mathbb{A}
, we shall call the presheaf
\nu_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{A})
the
(
\mathbb{C}
-)nerve
of
\mathbb{A}
.

Proof.

The adjunction
\int_{\mathbb{C}}\!\dashv\nu_{\mathbb{C}}
is an instance of the usual “realization/nerve” adjunction, here with respect to the covariant slice category functor
\mathbb{C}/-:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathsf{Cat}
, as indicated below.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{}\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Cat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{\mathbb{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{\mathbb{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}/_{-}}

(84)

In detail, for
\mathbb{A}\in\mathsf{Cat}
and
c\in\mathbb{C}
, let
\nu_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{A})(c)
be the Hom-set of functors,

\displaystyle\nu_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{A})(c)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Cat}\big({\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\,,\,\mathbb{A}\big)\,,

with contravariant action on
h:d\rightarrow c
given by pre-composing a functor
P:{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}
with the post-composition functor

{\mathbb{C}/_{h}}:{\mathbb{C}/_{d}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\,.

For the adjunction, observe that the slice category
\mathbb{C}/_{c}
is the category of elements of the representable functor
\mathsf{y}{c}
,

\textstyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}\mathsf{y}{c}\ \cong\ \mathbb{C}/_{c}\,.

Thus for representables
\mathsf{y}{c}
, we have the required natural isomorphism

\textstyle\widehat{}\mathbb{C}\big(\mathsf{y}{c}\,,\,\nu_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{A})\big)\ \cong\ \nu_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{A})(c)\ =\ \mathsf{Cat}\big({\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\,,\,\mathbb{A}\big)\ \cong\ \mathsf{Cat}\big(\int_{\mathbb{C}}\mathsf{y}{c}\,,\,\mathbb{A}\big)\,.

For arbitrary presheaves
X
, one uses the presentation of
X
as a colimit of representables over the index category
\int_{\mathbb{C}}X
, and the easy to prove fact that
\int_{\mathbb{C}}
itself preserves colimits. Indeed, for any category
\mathbb{D}
, we have an isomorphism in
\mathsf{Cat}
,

\varinjlim_{d\in\mathbb{D}}\,\mathbb{D}/_{d}\ \cong\ \mathbb{D}\,.

∎

When
\mathbb{C}
is fixed, we may omit the subscript in the notation
\mathsf{y}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
\int_{\mathbb{C}}
and
\nu_{\mathbb{C}}
. The unit and counit maps of the adjunction
\int\dashv\nu
,

\displaystyle\textstyle\eta:

\displaystyle\ \textstyle X\longrightarrow\nu{\textstyle\int\!{X}}\,,

\displaystyle\epsilon:

\displaystyle\ \textstyle\textstyle\int\!{\nu}\mathbb{A}\longrightarrow\mathbb{A}\,,

are then as follows. At
c\in\mathbb{C}
, for
x:\mathsf{y}{c}\rightarrow X
, the functor
(\eta_{X})_{c}(x):\mathbb{C}/_{c}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}/_{X}
is just composition with
x
,

(\eta_{X})_{c}(x)=\mathbb{C}/_{x}:\mathbb{C}/_{c}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}/_{X}\,.

(85)

For
\mathbb{A}\in\mathsf{Cat}
, the functor
\epsilon:\int\nu\mathbb{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}
takes a pair
(c\in\mathbb{C},f:\mathbb{C}/_{c}\rightarrow\mathbb{A})
to the object
f(1_{c})\in\mathbb{A}
,

\epsilon(c,f)=f(1_{c}).

Lemma 78
.

For any
f:Y\rightarrow X
, the naturality square below is a pullback.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\int\!{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\int\!{X}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\int\!{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{X}}

(86)

Proof.

It suffices to prove this for the case
f:X\rightarrow 1
. Thus consider the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\int\!{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\int\!1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{1}}

(87)

Evaluating at
c\in\mathbb{C}
and applying (
85
) gives the following square in
\mathsf{Set}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Xc}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Cat}\big({\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\,,\,{\mathbb{C}/_{X}}\,\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1c}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Cat}\big({\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\,,\,\mathbb{C}/_{1}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}/_{-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}/_{-}}

(88)

The image of
*\in 1c
along the bottom is the forgetful functor
U_{c}:\mathbb{C}/_{c}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, and its fiber under the map on the right is the set of functors
F:{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}/_{X}}
such that
U_{X}\circ F=U_{c}
, where
U_{X}:\mathbb{C}/_{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is also a forgetful functor. But any such
F
is uniquely of the form
\mathbb{C}/_{x}
for
x=F(1_{c}):\mathsf{y}{c}\rightarrow X
.
∎

A universal family.

For the terminal presheaf
1\in\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
we have an iso
\textstyle\int\!{1}\cong\mathbb{C}
, so for every
X\in\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
there is a canonical projection
\textstyle\int\!{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, which is a discrete fibration. It follows that for any map
Y\rightarrow X
of presheaves, the associated map
\textstyle\int\!{Y}\rightarrow\textstyle\int\!{X}
is also a discrete fibration.
Ignoring size issues temporarily, recall that discrete fibrations in
\mathsf{Cat}
are classified by the forgetful functor
{\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}
from (the opposites of) the category of pointed sets to that of sets (cf.
[
Web07
]
). For every presheaf
X\in\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
, we therefore have a pullback diagram in
\mathsf{Cat}
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\int\!{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

(89)

Using
\mathbb{C}\cong\textstyle\int\!{1}
and transposing by the adjunction
\int\dashv\nu
then gives a commutative square in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
of the form:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{X}}

(90)

Lemma 79
.

The square (
90
) is a pullback in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
. More generally, for any map
Y\rightarrow X
in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
, there is a canonical pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}\,.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

(91)

Proof.

Apply the right adjoint
\nu
to the pullback square (
89
) and paste the naturality square (
86
) from Lemma
78
on the left, to obtain the transposed square (
91
) as a pasting of two pullbacks.
∎

Let us write
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
for the vertical map on the right in (
91
), setting

\displaystyle\textstyle\dot{\mathcal{V}}\,

\displaystyle:=\,\nu{\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}

(92)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\,

\displaystyle:=\,\nu{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}.

We summarize our results so far as follows.

Proposition 80
.

The nerve
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
of the classifier for discrete fibrations
{\,\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}
, as defined in (
92
), classifies natural transformations
Y\rightarrow X
in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
, in the sense that there is always a pullback square,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Y}}

(93)

The classifying map
\tilde{Y}:X\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
is determined by the adjunction
\int\dashv\nu
as the transpose of the classifying map of the discrete fibration
\textstyle\int\!{Y}\rightarrow\textstyle\int\!{X}
.

Given a natural transformation
Y\rightarrow X
, the classifying map
\tilde{Y}:X\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
is of course not in general unique. Nonetheless, we can use the construction of
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
as the nerve of the discrete fibration classifier
{\,\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}
, for which classifying functors
\mathbb{C}\rightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}
are unique up to natural isomorphism, to infer the following proposition, which will be required below (cf.
[
Shu15
,
GSS22
]
).

Proposition 81

(Realignment for families)
.

Given a monomorphism
c:C\rightarrowtail X
and a family
Y\rightarrow X
, let
y_{c}:C\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
classify the pullback
c^{*}Y\rightarrow C
. Then there is a classifying map
y:X\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
for
Y\rightarrow X
with
y\circ c=y_{c}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{*}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}

(94)

Proof.

Transposing the realignment problem (
94
) for presheaves across the adjunction
\int\dashv\nu
results in the following realignment problem for discrete fibrations.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\int\!{c}^{*}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\int\!{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\int\!{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\int\!{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\int\!{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{y_{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{y}}

(95)

The category of elements functor
\int
is easily seen to preserve pullbacks, hence monos; thus let us consider the general case of a functor
C:\mathbb{C}\rightarrowtail\mathbb{D}
which is monic in
\mathsf{Cat}
, a pullback of discrete fibrations as on the left below, and a presheaf
E:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow{{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}
with
\textstyle\int\!{E}\cong\mathbb{E}
over
\mathbb{C}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

(96)

We seek
F:\mathbb{D}\rightarrow{{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}
with
\textstyle\int\!{F}\cong\mathbb{F}
over
\mathbb{D}
and
F\circ C=E
. Let
F_{0}:\mathbb{D}\rightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}
with
\textstyle\int\!{F}_{0}\cong\mathbb{F}
over
\mathbb{D}
, which exists since
\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}
is a discrete fibration. Since
F_{0}\circ C
and
E
both classify
\mathbb{E}
, there is a natural iso
e:F_{0}\circ C\cong E
.
Consider the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{(\mathsf{Set}^{\cong})}^{\mathrm{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

(97)

where
\mathsf{Set}^{\cong}
is the category of isos in
\mathsf{Set}
, with
p_{1},p_{2}
the (opposites of the) domain and codomain projections. There is a well-known weak factorization system on
\mathsf{Cat}
(part of the “canonical model structure”) with injective-on-objects functors on the left and isofibrations on the right. Thus there is a diagonal filler
f
as indicated. The functor
F:=p_{2}\circ f:\mathbb{D}\rightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}
is then the one we seek.
∎

Small maps.

Of course, as defined in (
92
), the classifier
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
cannot be a map in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
, for reasons of size; we now address this.
Let
\alpha
be a cardinal number, and call the sets strictly smaller than it
\alpha
-
small
. Let
\mathsf{Set}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Set}
be the full subcategory of
\alpha
-small sets.
Call a presheaf
X:{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathsf{Set}

\alpha
-small if all of its values are
\alpha
-small sets, and thus if, and only if, it factors through
\mathsf{Set}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Set}
. Call a map
f:Y\rightarrow X
of presheaves
\alpha
-small if all of the fibers
f_{c}^{-1}\{x\}\subseteq Yc
are
\alpha
-small sets (for all
c\in\mathbb{C}
and
x\in Xc
). The latter condition is of course equivalent to saying that, in the pullback square over the element
x:\mathsf{y}{c}\rightarrow X
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{y}{c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}

(98)

the presheaf
Y_{x}
is
\alpha
-small.

Now let us restrict the specification (
92
) of
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
to the
\alpha
-small sets:

\displaystyle\textstyle\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha}\,

\displaystyle:=\,\nu\dot{\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\alpha}}

(99)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}\,

\displaystyle:=\,\nu\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\alpha}.

Then the evident forgetful map
\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}

is
a map in the category
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
of presheaves, and it is in fact
\alpha
-small. Moreover, it has the following basic property, which is just a restriction of the basic property of
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
stated in Proposition
80
.

Proposition 82
.

The map
\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}
classifies
\alpha
-small maps
f:Y\rightarrow X
in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
, in the sense that there is always a pullback square,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Y}}

(100)

The classifying map
\tilde{Y}:X\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}
is determined by the adjunction
\int\dashv\nu
as (the factorization of) the transpose of the classifiyng map of the discrete fibration
\textstyle\int\!{X}\rightarrow\textstyle\int\!{Y}
.

Proof.

If
Y\rightarrow X
is
\alpha
-small, its classifying map
\tilde{Y}:X\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
factors through
\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}
, as indicated below,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\dot{\mathsf{Set}_{\alpha}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\mathsf{Set}_{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Y}}

(101)

in virtue of the following adjoint transposition,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\int\!{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dot{\mathsf{Set}_{\alpha}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dot{\mathsf{Set}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\int\!{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Set}_{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathrm{op}}.}

(102)

Note that the square on the right is evidently a pullback, and so the one on the left is, too, because the outer rectangle is the classifying pulback of the discrete fibration
\textstyle\int\!{Y}\rightarrow\textstyle\int\!{X}
, as stated. Thus the left square in (
101
) is also a pullback.
∎

Examples of universal families
\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}
.

(1)

Let
\alpha=\kappa
a strongly inaccessible cadinal, so that
\mathsf{ob}({\mathsf{Set}_{\kappa}})
is a Grothendieck universe. Then the Hofmann-Streicher universe of Definition
76
is recovered as the
\kappa
-small map classifier

E\,\cong\,\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}\,\cong\,U

in the sense of Proposition
82
. Indeed, for
c\in\mathbb{C}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}{c}\

\displaystyle=\ \nu(\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa})(c)=\mathsf{Cat}\big({\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\,,\,\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa}\big)\ =\ \mathsf{ob}(\widehat{\mathbb{C}/_{c}})\ =\ U{c}\,.

(103)

For
\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}
we then have,

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}{c}\ =\ \nu(\,\dot{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa})(c)\

\displaystyle=\ \mathsf{Cat}\big({\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\,,\,\,\dot{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa}\big)

\displaystyle\cong\ {\textstyle\coprod_{A\in\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}{c}}\mathsf{Cat}_{{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}}\big({\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\,,\,A^{*}\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa}\big)}

(104)

where the
A
-summand in (
1
) is defined by taking sections of the pullback indicated below.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{*}\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,\dot{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}/_{c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}

(105)

But
A^{*}\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa}\ \cong\ {\textstyle\int_{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\!A}
over
\mathbb{C}/_{c}\,
, and sections of this discrete fibration in
\mathsf{Cat}
correspond uniquely to natural maps
1\rightarrow A
in
\widehat{{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}}
. Since
1
is representable in
\widehat{{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}}
we can continue (
1
) by

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\kappa}{c}\

\displaystyle\cong\ {\textstyle\coprod_{A\in\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}{c}}\mathsf{Cat}_{{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}}\big({\mathbb{C}/_{c}}\,,\,A^{*}\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{\kappa}\big)}

\displaystyle\cong\ {\textstyle\coprod_{A\in\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}{c}}\widehat{{\mathbb{C}/_{c}}}(1,A)}

\displaystyle\cong\ {\textstyle\coprod_{A\in\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}{c}}A(1_{c})}

\displaystyle=\ {\textstyle\coprod_{A\in\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}{c}}{\mathsf{E}l}(\langle c,A\rangle)}

\displaystyle=\ Ec\,.

(2)

By functoriality of the nerve
\nu:\mathsf{Cat}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
, a sequence of Grothendieck universes

\mathsf{Set}_{\alpha}\subseteq\mathsf{Set}_{\beta}\subseteq...

in
\mathsf{Set}
gives rise to a (cumulative) sequence of type-theoretic universes

\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}\rightarrowtail{\mathcal{V}_{\beta}}\rightarrowtail...

in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
. More precisely, there is a sequence of cartesian squares,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\beta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{V}_{\beta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots\,,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

(106)

in the image of
\nu:\mathsf{Cat}\longrightarrow\widehat{}\mathbb{C}
, classifying small maps in
\widehat{}\mathbb{C}
of increasing size, in the sense of Proposition
82
.

(3)

Let
\alpha=2
so that
1\rightarrow 2
is the subobject classifier of
\mathsf{Set}
, and

{\mathbb{1}}=\,\dot{\mathsf{Set}}^{\mathsf{op}}_{2}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}}_{2}=\mathbbm{2}

is then a classifier in
\mathsf{Cat}
for
sieves
, i.e. full subcategories
\mathbb{S}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{A}
closed under the domains of arrows
a\rightarrow s
for
s\in\mathbb{S}
. The nerve
\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{2}
is then the usual subobject classifier
1\rightarrow\Omega
of
\widehat{}\mathbb{C}
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu\mathbbm{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu\mathbbm{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

(107)

(4)

For any
X\in\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
, we have an equivalence

\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/_{X}\ \simeq\ \widehat{\textstyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}X}\ \simeq\ \mathsf{dFib}/_{\!\int_{\mathbb{C}}X}

where, generally,
\mathsf{dFib}/_{\mathbb{D}}
is the category of discrete fibrations over a category
\mathbb{D}
.
This equivalence commutes with composition along discrete fibrations, in the sense that the forgetful functor

{X_{!}}:\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/_{X}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbb{C}}

given by composition along
X\rightarrow 1
agrees (up to canonical isomorphism) with the base change
(p_{X})_{!}\dashv(p_{X})^{*}
of presheaves along the projection
{\textstyle p_{X}:\int_{\mathbb{C}}X\rightarrow\mathbb{C}}
, and with composition along the discrete fibration
p_{X}
, as indicated in:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\textstyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dFib}/_{\!\int_{\mathbb{C}}X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dFib}/_{\!\mathbb{C}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{!}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(p_{X})_{!}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{X}\circ(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

(108)

It follows that the pullback functor
X^{*}:\widehat{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/_{X}
commutes with the corresponding right adjoints (one of which is the nerve), and therefore preserves the respective universes,

\textstyle X^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{C}}\ \cong\ (p_{X})^{*}\nu_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}})\ \cong\ \nu_{\int_{\mathbb{C}}X}(\mathsf{Set}^{\mathsf{op}})\ \cong\ \mathcal{V}_{\int_{\mathbb{C}}X}\,.

Corollary 83
.

Let
\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}
classify
\alpha
-small maps in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
, as in Proposition
82
. Then for any
X\in\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
, the pullback
X^{*}\dot{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha}\rightarrow X^{*}\mathcal{V}_{\alpha}
classifies

\alpha
-small maps in
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/_{X}
.

Classifying trivial fibrations

Returning now to the presheaf category
\mathsf{cSet}=\mathsf{Set}^{\Box^{\mathrm{op}}}
of cubical sets, recall from section
2
that (uniform) trivial fibration structures on a map
A\rightarrow X
correspond bijectively to relative +-algebra structures over
X
(definition
13
). A relative
+
-algebra structure on
A\rightarrow X
is an algebra structure for the pointed polynomial endofunctor
+_{X}:\mathsf{cSet}/X\longrightarrow\mathsf{cSet}/X
, where recall from (
2
),

A^{+}=\sum_{\varphi:\Phi}A^{[\varphi]}\quad\text{over $X$}.

A +-algebra structure is then a retract
\alpha:A^{+}\rightarrow A
over
X
of the canonical map
\eta_{A}:A\rightarrow A^{+}
,

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{A}}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{A^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces A}
\textstyle{X.}

(109)

In more detail, let us write
A\rightarrow X
as a family
(A_{x})_{x\in X}
, so that
A=\sum_{x:X}A_{x}\rightarrow X
. Since the +-functor acts fiberwise, the object
A^{+}
in (
109
) is then the indexing projection

\sum_{x:X}A^{+}_{x}\rightarrow X.

Working in the slice
\mathsf{cSet}/X
, the (relative) exponentials (internal Hom’s)
[A^{+},A]
and
[A,A]
and the “precomposition by
\eta_{A}
” map
[\eta_{A},A]
, fit into the following pullback diagram

\textstyle{+\mathsf{Alg}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[A^{+},A]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\eta_{A},A]}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}^{\prime}{\mathsf{id}_{A}}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{[A,A].}

(110)

The constructed object
+\mathsf{Alg}(A)\rightarrow X
over
X
is then the
object of +-algebra structures on
A\rightarrow X
, in the sense that sections
X\rightarrow+\mathsf{Alg}(A)
correspond uniquely to +-algebra structures on
A\rightarrow X
. Moreover,
+\mathsf{Alg}(A)\rightarrow X
is stable under pullback, in the sense that for any
f:Y\rightarrow X
, we have two pullback squares,

\textstyle{f^{*}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{+\mathsf{Alg}(f^{*}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{+\mathsf{Alg}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(111)

because the +-functor, exponentials and pullbacks occurring in the construction of
+\mathsf{Alg}(A)\rightarrow X
are themselves all stable.

It then follows from Proposition
82
that, if
A\rightarrow X
is small, then
+\mathsf{Alg}(A)\rightarrow X
is itself a pullback of the analogous object
+\mathsf{Alg}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
constructed from the universal small family
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
of Proposition
82
, so there are two pullback squares:

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\chi_{A}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}}
\textstyle{+\mathsf{Alg}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{+\mathsf{Alg}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(112)

Proposition 84
.

There is a
universal small trivial fibration

\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}.

Every small trivial fibration
A\rightarrow X
is a pullback of
\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}
along a canonically determined classifying map
X\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}
.

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TFib}}

(113)

Proof.

We can take

\mathsf{TFib}:=+\mathsf{Alg}(\dot{\mathcal{V}}),

which comes with its projection
+\mathsf{Alg}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
as in diagram (
112
). Now define
p_{t}:\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}
by pulling back the universal small family,

\textstyle{\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{t}}
\textstyle{\dot{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TFib}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}.}

Consider the following diagram, in which all the squares (including the distorted ones) are pullbacks, with the outer one coming from proposition
82
and the lower one from (
112
).

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{A}}
\textstyle{\dot{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{t}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TFib}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\mathsf{TFib}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\chi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}.}

(114)

A trivial fibration structure
\alpha
on
A\rightarrow X
is a section the object of
+
-algebra structures on
A
, occurring in the diagram as

\mathsf{TFib}(A):=+\mathsf{Alg}(A),

the pullback of
\mathsf{TFib}=+\mathsf{Alg}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})
along the classifying map
\chi_{A}:X\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
for the small family
A\rightarrow X
. Such sections correspond uniquely to factorizations
\alpha^{\prime}
of
\chi_{A}
as indicated, which in turn induce pullback squares of the required kind (
113
).

Note that the map
p_{t}:\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}
has a canonical trivial fibration structure. Indeed, consider the following diagram, in which both squares are pullbacks.

\textstyle{\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{t}}
\textstyle{\dot{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TFib}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathsf{TFib}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(115)

\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})
is the object of trivial fibration structures on
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
, and its pullback
\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathsf{TFib}})
is therefore the object of trivial fibration structures on
p_{t}:\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}
. Thus we seek a section of
\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathsf{TFib}})\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}
. But recall that
\mathsf{TFib}=\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})
by definition, so the lower pullback square is the pullback of
\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
against itself, which does indeed have a distinguished section, namely the diagonal

\Delta:\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})\times_{\mathcal{V}}\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathcal{V}}).

∎

We record the following notation and corresponding fact from the foregoing proof for future reference:

Lemma 85
.

The classifying type
\mathsf{TFib}(A):=+\mathsf{Alg}(A)\rightarrow X
for trivial fibration structures on a map
A\rightarrow X
is stable under pullback, in the sense that for any
f:Y\rightarrow X
, we have two pullback squares,

\textstyle{f^{*}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TFib}(f^{*}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{TFib}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(116)

Since the universal small trivial fibration
\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}
in
\mathsf{cSet}
from Proposition
84
was constructed as
\mathsf{TFib}=\mathsf{TFib}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})
for the universal small family
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
, which in turn is stable under pullback by Corollary
83
, we also have:

Corollary 86
.

The base change of the universal small trivial fibration

\dot{\mathsf{TFib}}\rightarrow\mathsf{TFib}

in
\mathsf{cSet}
along
\mathrm{I}^{*}:\mathsf{cSet}\rightarrow\mathsf{cSet}/_{\mathrm{I}}
is a universal small trivial fibration in
\mathsf{cSet}/_{\mathrm{I}}
.

Classifying fibrations

In order to classify fibrations
A\twoheadrightarrow X
, we shall proceed as for trivial fibrations by constructing, for any map
A\rightarrow X
, an object
\mathsf{Fib}(A)\rightarrow X
of fibration structures which, moreover, is stable under pullback. We then apply the construction to the universal small family
\dot{\mathcal{V}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
of Proposition
82
to obtain a universal small fibration. Here we will of course need to distinguish between biased and unbiased fibrations. In Lemma
87
, we first construct a stable classifying type
\mathsf{Fib}(A)\rightarrow X
for
\delta
-biased fibration structures on any map
A\rightarrow X
in
\mathsf{cSet}/_{\mathrm{I}}
where
\delta
is the generic point. In Lemma
90

we then transfer the construction along the base change
\mathrm{I}^{*}:\mathsf{cSet}\rightarrow\mathsf{cSet}/_{\mathrm{I}}
to obtain a classifier
\mathsf{Fib}(A)\rightarrow X
for unbiased fibration structures on any
A\rightarrow X
in
\mathsf{cSet}
.

The construction of
\mathsf{Fib}(A)\rightarrow X
for biased fibration structures with respect to a point
\delta:1\rightarrow\mathrm{I}
is already a bit more involved than was that of
\mathsf{TFib}(A)\rightarrow X
. In particular, it requires the codomain
\mathrm{I}
of
\delta
to be
tiny
, which is indeed the case for the generic point
\delta:1\rightarrow\mathrm{I}^{*}\mathrm{I}
in
\mathsf{cSet}/_{\mathrm{I}}
by Lemma
8
.

The classifying type of biased fibration structures.

A classifying type
\mathsf{Fib}(A)\rightarrow X
of (uniform,
\delta
-biased) fibration structures on a map
p:A\rightarrow X
,
as defined in Section
3
, can be constructed as follows.

(1)

First form the pullback-hom
\delta\!\Rightarrow\!p:A^{\mathrm{I}}\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{I}}\times_{X}A
with the point
\delta:1\rightarrow\mathrm{I}
, as indicated in the following diagram.

\textstyle{A^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(p_{A})^{\mathrm{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\delta\Rightarrow{p}}
\scriptstyle{A^{\delta}}
\textstyle{X^{\mathrm{I}}\times_{X}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{X^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X^{\delta}}
\textstyle{X}

(117)

(2)

A fibration structure on
p:A\rightarrow X
is then a relative +-algebra structure on
\delta\Rightarrow p
in the slice category over its codomain
X^{\mathrm{I}}\times_{X}A
.
To construct a classifier for such structures, let us first relabel the objects and arrows in diagram (
117
) as follows:

\displaystyle\epsilon

\displaystyle:=X^{\delta}:X^{\mathrm{I}}\rightarrow X

\displaystyle A_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:=X^{\mathrm{I}}\times_{X}A

\displaystyle\epsilon_{A}

\displaystyle:=\delta\Rightarrow p

so that the working part of (
117
) becomes:

\textstyle{A^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{A}}
\textstyle{A_{\epsilon}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{\epsilon}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{X^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\textstyle{X}

(118)

(3)

Now a relative +-algebra structure on
\epsilon_{A}
(Definition
13
) is a retract
\alpha
over
A_{\epsilon}
of the unit
\eta
, as indicated below, where
D
is simply the domain of the map
(\epsilon_{A})^{+}
resulting from applying the relative +-functor in the slice category over
A_{\epsilon}
to the object
\epsilon_{A}
.

\textstyle{A^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{(\epsilon_{A})^{+}}
\textstyle{A_{\epsilon}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{\epsilon}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{X^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\textstyle{X}

(119)

(4)

As in the construction (
110
), there is an object
\mathsf{TFib}(\epsilon_{A})=+\mathsf{Alg}(\epsilon_{A})
over
A_{\epsilon}
of relative +-algebra structures on
\epsilon_{A}
, the sections of which correspond uniquely to relative +-algebra structures on
\epsilon_{A}
(and thus to fibration structures on

).

\textstyle{A^{\mathrm{I}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

Remark 89
.

Proposition
88
made no use of the fact that we were working in the slice category
\mathsf{cSet}/_{\mathrm{I}}
with
\delta:1\rightarrow\mathrm{I}
the generic point. It holds equally for
\delta
-biased fibrations with respect to any point
\delta:1\rightarrow\mathrm{I}
of a tiny object
\mathrm{I}
. Thus
e.g.
it could be used (with obvious adjustment) to construct a classifier for the
\{\delta_{0},\delta_{1}\}
-biased fibrations of Section
3
in (Cartesian, Dedekind, or other varieties of) cubical sets
\mathsf{cSet}
.

The classifying type of unbiased fibration structures.

In order to classify
unbiased
fibration structures on maps
A\rightarrow X
in
\mathsf{cSet}
, we first apply the pullback
\mathrm{I}^{*}:\mathsf{cSet}\rightarrow\mathsf{cSet}/_{\mathrm{I}}
and take the classifier
\mathsf{Fib}(\mathrm{I}^{*}A)\rightarrow\mathrm{I}^{*}X
for
\delta
-biased fibration structures, then apply the pushforward
\mathrm{I}_{*}:\mathsf{cSet}/_{\mathrm{I}}\rightarrow\mathsf{cSet}
and pull the result
\mathrm{I}_{*}\mathsf{Fib}(\mathrm{I}^{*}A)\rightarrow\mathrm{I}_{*}\mathrm{I}^{*}X
back along the unit
X\rightarrow\mathrm{I}_{*}\mathrm{I}^{*}X
.

To show that this indeed classifies unbiased fibration structures on
A\rightarrow X
, let us first rename the classifying type from Lemma
87
, which was constructed over
\mathrm{I}
, to
\mathsf{Fib}_{i}(\mathrm{I}^{*}A)\rightarrow\mathrm{I}^{*}X
, and then apply
\mathrm{I}_{*}
to get the map,

\Pi_{i:\mathrm{I}}\mathsf{Fib}_{i}(\mathrm{I}^{*}A):=\mathrm{I}_{*}(\mathsf{Fib}_{i}(\mathrm{I}^{*}A))\longrightarrow X^{\mathrm{I}}

in
\mathsf{cSet}
. Then, as just said, we define the desired map
\mathsf{Fib}(A)\rightarrow X
as the pullback along the unit
\rho:X\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{I}}
of
\mathrm{I}^{*}\dashv\mathrm{I}_{*}
as indicated below.

\textstyle{\mathsf{Fib}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Pi_{i:\mathrm{I}}\mathsf{Fib}_{i}(\mathrm{I}^{*}A)}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{X^{\mathrm{I}}}

(130)

It now follows immediately from the adjunction
\mathrm{I}^{*}\dashv\mathrm{I}_{*}
that sections of
\mathsf{Fib}(A)\rightarrow X
correspond bijectively to sections of
\mathsf{Fib}_{i}(\mathrm{I}^{*}A)\rightarrow\mathrm{I}^{*}X
over
\mathrm{I}
, and thus to
unbiased
fibration structures on
A\rightarrow X
.

Lemma 90
.

For any map
A\rightarrow X
in
\mathsf{cSet}
, the map
\mathsf{Fib}(A)\rightarrow X
in (
130
) is a
classifying type for unbiased fibration structures
: sections of
\mathsf{Fib}(A)\rightarrow X
correspond bijectively to unbiased fibration structures on
A\rightarrow X
, and the construction is stable under pullback in the expected sense (as in Lemma
87
).

Proof.

It remains only to check the stability, but since both of the adjoints in
\mathrm{I}^{*}\dashv\mathrm{I}_{*}:\mathsf{cSet}/_{\mathrm{I}}\rightarrow\mathsf{cSet}
preserve pullbacks, this follows easily from the fact that the classifying types
\mathsf{Fib}_{i}
are stable under pullback by Lemma
87
.
∎

Finally, we can again take
\mathsf{Fib}:=\mathsf{Fib}(\dot{\mathcal{V}})
to now obtain a universal small
unbiased

in
\mathsf{cSet}

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Fib}}

(131)

Remark 92
.

Recall from Proposition

that the universe in the slice category

is the pullback of the universe

from

\mathsf{Fib}_{i}(\mathrm{I}^{*}\dot{\mathcal{V}})\rightarrow\mathrm{I}^{*}\mathcal{V}

q:\mathrm{I}^{*}\mathcal{V}=\mathrm{I}\times\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}

\textstyle{\mathsf{Fib}_{i}(\mathrm{I}^{*}\dot{\mathcal{V}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
